<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove a mod-<i>ℓ</i> Bogomolov–Pop reconstruction theorem for function fields of transcendence degree at least two over arbitrary algebraically closed fields of characteristic different from <i>ℓ</i>. The groups $`K^{\mathrm M}_1/\ell`$ and $`K^{\mathrm M}_2/\ell`$, together with their full bilinear product, determine the perfect closure and its constant field. Every compatible isomorphism of these data is induced by a field isomorphism up to a single scalar in $`\mathbb F_\ell^\times`$, with only Frobenius ambiguity in the field isomorphism in positive characteristic.

- **claim-main-reconstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $\ell$ be any prime. Let $K/k$ and $L/l$ be finitely generated extensions of arbitrary algebraically closed fields, with $\mathop{\mathrm{char}}k,\mathop{\mathrm{char}}l\ne\ell$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(K/k),\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(L/l)\ge2$. Then <a href="#eq:main-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main-map">[eq:main-map]</a> is bijective. In particular, a compatible isomorphism $V_K\to V_L$ forces equality of the characteristics and of the relative transcendence degrees.
  - Explanation: For finitely generated extensions of algebraically closed fields with transcendence degree at least two and characteristic different from the prime, the canonical map from field isomorphisms of perfect closures (modulo Frobenius) to compatible isomorphisms of the Milnor data (modulo scalars) is bijective.
- **claim-local-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-local-input)): Let $F$ be a field and set $A_F^{\pm}=\{f\in A_F:f(-1)=0\}$.* 1. *Every valuative subset $\Sigma\subset A_F$ has a unique coarsest valuation $v_\Sigma$ with $\Sigma\subset I_{v_\Sigma}$. In particular, $v_\Sigma$ coarsens every valuation whose inertia contains $\Sigma$. For such a valuation $w$, this coarsening is obtained by quotienting $wF$ by its largest convex subgroup contained in $w(\bigcap_{f\in\Sigma}\ker f)$.* 2. *If $S\subset A_F^{\pm}$ is an alternating linear subspace, its valuative elements form a valuative subspace $S_0$ of codimension at most one. Moreover $S\subset D_{v_{S_0}}$.* 3. *If $\Sigma\subset A_F^{\pm}$ is valuative and $g\in A_F^{\pm}$ is alternating with every element of $\Sigma$, then $g\in D_{v_\Sigma}$.
  - Explanation: Imported alternating-pair valuation theory: valuative subsets have a unique coarsest valuation whose inertia contains them, alternating subspaces have a valuative part of small codimension, and characters alternating with a valuative set lie in the corresponding decomposition space.
- **claim-local-abhyankar** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-abhyankar)): For every valuation $v$ of $F$, the field $\kappa v$ is algebraically closed, the group $vF/v\kappa$ is torsion free, and $$\mathop{\mathrm{rrank}}(vF/v\kappa)+\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(Fv/\kappa v)\leq s. \label{eq:local-abhyankar}$$ Here $\mathop{\mathrm{rrank}}(G)=\dim_\mathbb Q(G\otimes_\mathbb Z\mathbb Q)$. If equality holds, then $Fv/\kappa v$ is finitely generated and $vF/v\kappa$ is a finitely generated free abelian group. Equality passes to every coarsening and to the induced residue valuation. Conversely, equality for a valuation and an induced residue valuation gives equality for their composition.
  - Explanation: Every valuation has algebraically closed residue constants and a torsion-free relative value group, and the rational rank of the latter plus the residue transcendence degree is bounded by the ambient transcendence degree, with equality giving finite generation and stability under coarsening and composition.
- **claim-residue-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-residue-duality)): Suppose $vF/v\kappa$ is free abelian of finite rank. Then restriction to units induces a canonical topological isomorphism $$D_v/I_v\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }A_{Fv}. \label{eq:local-residue-duality}$$ Two elements of $D_v$ are alternating if and only if their images in $A_{Fv}$ are alternating. If $w$ is a valuation of $Fv$, then $$I_{w\circ v}=\rho_v^{-1}(I_w),\qquad D_{w\circ v}=\rho_v^{-1}(D_w), \label{eq:local-composition}$$ where the inverse images are taken inside $D_v$.
  - Explanation: When the relative value group is free of finite rank, restriction to units identifies the quotient of decomposition by inertia with the character space of the residue field, preserves alternating pairs, and gives the composition formulas for inertia and decomposition.
- **claim-local-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-approximation)): Let $v_1,v_2$ be incomparable valuations of a field, and let $u$ be their finest common coarsening. Then $$U_{v_1}^1U_{v_2}^1=U_u, \qquad D_{v_1}\cap D_{v_2}=I_u.$$
  - Explanation: For incomparable valuations, the product of their principal unit groups is the unit group of the finest common coarsening, so the intersection of decomposition spaces equals that coarsening's inertia.
- **claim-local-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-local-dimension)): The maximum dimension of an alternating subspace of $A_F$ is $s$. If $S\subset A_F$ is alternating of dimension $s$, and $v$ is the valuation associated to its valuative part $S_0$, then $S_0=I_v$ and equality holds in <a href="#eq:local-abhyankar" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-abhyankar">[eq:local-abhyankar]</a> for $v$.
  - Explanation: The transcendence degree is the largest dimension of an alternating subspace of the character space, and a subspace attaining it has inertia as valuative part with equality in the valuation inequality.
- **claim-quasi-recovery** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-quasi-recovery)): Assume $s\geq2$. A line $H\subset A_F$ is the inertia of a quasi-divisorial valuation if and only if $$H=S_1\cap S_2 \quad\text{for alternating subspaces }S_1,S_2\subset A_F \text{ of dimension }s. \label{eq:local-inertia-intersection}$$ The quasi-divisorial valuation $v$ with $I_v=H$ is unique. For any nonzero $h\in H$, its decomposition space is $$D_v=\{g\in A_F:(h,g)\text{ is alternating}\}. \label{eq:local-decomposition-recognition}$$
  - Explanation: A line of characters is the inertia of a quasi-divisorial valuation exactly when it is the intersection of two maximal alternating subspaces; that valuation is unique and its decomposition space is read off by alternation with any generator.
- **claim-local-flag-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-flag-uniqueness)): If $v$ is a quasi-prime $r$-divisor, with $r\geq1$, then its value group has no nonzero $\ell$-divisible convex subgroup. Moreover $v=v_{I_v}$; in particular its inertia determines the valuation.
  - Explanation: A composite quasi-prime flag has no nonzero divisible convex subgroup in its value group, hence the valuation is determined by its inertia.
- **claim-flag-matching** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-flag-matching)): For the fields $K/k,L/l$ of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, a compatible isomorphism $\Theta$ forces $\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(K/k)=\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(L/l)=d$, say. Its dual $\Phi$ matches the inertia–decomposition pairs of quasi-prime divisors, and, successively, the pairs of quasi-prime $r$-divisors for $1\leq r\leq d-1$. For a matched pair of valuations $v$ on $K$ and $w$ on $L$, it induces a topological linear isomorphism $$A_{Lw}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }A_{Kv}$$ preserving alternating pairs in both directions.
  - Explanation: A compatible isomorphism preserves transcendence degree and matches inertia–decomposition pairs of successive quasi-prime divisors, inducing alternating-preserving isomorphisms of residue character spaces.
- **claim-density-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-density-input)): Let $F/\kappa$ be a function field over an algebraically closed field of characteristic different from $\ell$. Suppose that a valuation $v$ of $F$, with $Fv/\kappa v$ a function field, has the following properties:* 1. *$vF$ has no nonzero $\ell$-divisible convex subgroup;* 2. *there is a subfield $\kappa\subset\kappa_1\subset F$ such that $$\kappa_1v=\kappa v,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(F/\kappa_1)=\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(Fv/\kappa_1v)=1.$$* *Then $\widehat J_v$ contains the full minimized inertia group $\widehat I_u$ of every c.r. quasi-prime divisor $u$ of $Fv/\kappa v$.
  - Explanation: Imported density statement: under the stated valuation hypotheses, the closed union of quasi-prime inertias, restricted to the decomposition space and reduced to the residue field, contains the full minimized inertia of every constant-reduction quasi-prime divisor.
- **claim-residual-density-mod-ell** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-residual-density-mod-ell)): For a quasi-prime $(d-1)$-divisor $v$ of $F/\kappa$, the set $J_v$ contains the inertia line $I_u\subset A_{F_0}$ of every c.r. quasi-prime divisor $u$ of $F_0/\kappa_0$.
  - Explanation: Reducing characters modulo the prime transfers the density input, so the residual inertia set contains the inertia line of every constant-reduction quasi-prime divisor of the residue field.
- **claim-residual-valuative** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-residual-valuative)): Every element of $S_F^{\mathrm{in}}$ is valuative. Every element of $J_v$ is valuative as a character of $F_0$.
  - Explanation: The closed union of inertia spaces consists of valuative characters, and so do the residual images.
- **claim-curve-orders** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-curve-orders)): Let $B/b$ be a function field of transcendence degree one over an algebraically closed field, and let $C/b$ be its smooth projective curve. For $x\in C(b)$, let $\delta_x=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_x\bmod\ell\in A_B$. The lines $\Lambda\delta_x$ are distinct and nonzero. The family $(\delta_x)_{x\in C(b)}$ tends to zero outside finite subsets, and the continuous summation map $$\label{eq:curve-summation} \prod_{x\in C(b)}\Lambda\longrightarrow A_B,\qquad (a_x)_x\longmapsto\sum_x a_x\delta_x$$ has kernel consisting exactly of the constant families. If $\mathop{\mathrm{char}}(b)\ne\ell$, the common kernel in $V_B$ of the $\delta_x$ has dimension $2g(C)$.
  - Explanation: On a smooth projective curve the point-order characters give distinct nonzero lines tending to zero, their summation map has only constant families as kernel, and the common kernel in the multiplicative classes has dimension given by twice the genus away from the prime characteristic.
- **claim-point-recognition** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-point-recognition)): For a quasi-prime $(d-1)$-divisor $v$ of $F/\kappa$, the family $\mathcal L_v$ satisfies the preceding two conditions if and only if $\kappa v$ is algebraic over a finite field. When this holds, $\mathcal L_v$ consists exactly of the point inertia lines of the smooth projective curve of $Fv/\kappa v$. Consequently the matching in Corollary <a href="#cor:flag-matching" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:flag-matching">10</a> recognizes these terminal valuations and matches their full point families.
  - Explanation: The two convergence and kernel conditions on the family of residual inertia lines hold exactly when the residue constants are algebraic over a finite field, in which case the lines are the point lines of the residual curve.
- **claim-kummer-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-kummer-kernel)): Let $E/F$ be a finitely generated field extension, where $\mathop{\mathrm{char}}F\ne\ell$ and $\mu_\ell\subset F$. The kernel of $V_F\longrightarrow V_E$ is finite dimensional over $\Lambda$.
  - Explanation: Under a finitely generated extension containing the roots of unity, only a finite-dimensional space of multiplicative classes can become trivial.
- **claim-support-bertini** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-support-bertini)): Let $U$ be a smooth integral locally closed subvariety of projective space over an algebraically closed field, of dimension at least two. Let $Y\to U$ be a finite étale morphism with $Y$ integral. For general hyperplanes $H$, both $U\cap H$ and $Y\times_U(U\cap H)$ are nonempty, smooth, and integral.
  - Explanation: A general hyperplane section of a smooth integral variety and of an integral finite étale cover of it remain nonempty, smooth and integral.
- **claim-finite-curve-test** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-curve-test)): There is an integer $G_X\ge0$, depending only on the embedded model $X$, with the following property. Given $g_1,\ldots,g_m\in L^\times$ whose classes in $V_L$ are independent, there is a quasi-prime $(d-1)$-divisor $w$ of $L/l$ such that:* 1. *$lw$ is algebraically closed and algebraic over a finite field of characteristic different from $\ell$, and $Lw$ is the function field of a smooth projective curve $C_w/lw$ of genus at most $G_X$;* 2. *the $g_j$ are $w$-units and their residue classes are independent in $V_{Lw}$;* 3. *for every $j$, $$\#\{x\in C_w(lw):\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_x(\overline{g_j})\not\equiv0\pmod\ell\} =s_X([g_j]).$$
  - Explanation: For independent classes there is a quasi-prime divisor whose residual curve has constants algebraic over a finite field, genus bounded in terms of the model only, preserves independence and realizes the support degrees as counts of nonzero point orders.
- **claim-uniform-support** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-uniform-support)): For every $t\in K\setminus k$, $$\sup_{a\in k}s_X\bigl(\Theta([t-a])\bigr)<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: For a nonconstant function, the degrees of the mod-prime supports of the transported pencil classes are bounded independently of the pencil member.
- **claim-constant-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-constant-descent)): Let $F/B$ be a finitely generated regular extension, where $\mathop{\mathrm{char}}(B)\ne\ell$ and $\mu_\ell\subset B$. If $g\in F^\times$ has an $\ell$-th root in $F\overline B$, then its class in $V_F$ lies in the image of $V_B$.
  - Explanation: In a regular extension, a class acquiring a root after adjoining algebraic constants already comes from the base field, by a cocycle argument.
- **claim-curve-intersection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-curve-intersection)): Let $F/\kappa$ be a finitely generated extension of an algebraically closed field of characteristic different from $\ell$. If $P,Q\subset F$ are distinct curve subfields, then $$\dim_\Lambda(V_P\cap V_Q)<\infty.$$ Each $V_P$ is infinite dimensional. In particular, an inclusion $V_P\subset V_Q$ forces $P=Q$.
  - Explanation: Distinct curve subfields share only a finite-dimensional space of classes while each carries an infinite-dimensional space, so inclusion of class spaces forces equality of the subfields.
- **claim-curve-incidence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-curve-incidence)): Let $X\subset\mathbb P_l^n$ be a normal integral projective variety of dimension $d\ge2$ over an algebraically closed field $l$ of characteristic different from $\ell$, and put $L=l(X)$. Let $\mathcal S$ be an infinite set of prime divisors on $X$ whose degrees are bounded. Suppose that $H\subset V_L$ is a subspace with the following property: for every $g\in L^\times$ with $[g]\in H$, $$\label{eq:curve-residue-trivial} \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_D(g)=0\quad\hbox{and}\quad g\|_D\in l(D)^{\times\ell} \qquad\hbox{for all but finitely many }D\in\mathcal S.$$ Then there are a finite extension $M/L$ and a one-variable field $P_0/l$ contained in $M$ such that $M/P_0$ is regular, $$\label{eq:curve-compositum} M=LP_0,$$ and the image of $H$ in $V_M$ is contained in the image of $V_{P_0}$. In particular, every $g$ as above has an $\ell$-th root in $M\overline{P_0}$.
  - Explanation: A subspace of classes that are trivial in order and become powers on almost all divisors of a bounded infinite family descends to a one-variable field inside a finite extension that is a compositum with the original field.
- **claim-curve-matching** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-curve-matching)): For every curve subfield $E\subset K$ there is a unique curve subfield $P\subset L$ such that $\Theta(V_E)=V_P$. This assignment is a bijection between the curve subfields of $K/k$ and those of $L/l$.
  - Explanation: The compatible isomorphism induces a bijection between curve subfields of the two function fields matching their class spaces.
- **claim-true-divisors** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-true-divisors)): Let $F/\kappa$ be one of $K/k$ or $L/l$. A quasi-divisorial valuation $v$ of $F/\kappa$ is a true prime divisor if and only if there is a curve subfield $E\subset F$ for which $$\label{eq:good-true-test} \dim_\Lambda(V_E\cap D_v^\perp)<\infty.$$ Consequently $\Phi$ matches the inertia and decomposition groups of true prime divisors of $L/l$ with those of $K/k$.
  - Explanation: A quasi-divisorial valuation is trivial on the constants exactly when some curve subfield meets the annihilator of its decomposition space in finite dimension, so the matching respects true divisors.
- **claim-good-matching** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-good-matching)): The curve-subfield correspondence of Theorem <a href="#thm:curve-matching" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:curve-matching">23</a> restricts to a bijection on good subfields. For matched good subfields $E\subset K$ and $P\subset L$, the induced character isomorphism gives a bijection between the points of their smooth projective rational curves.
  - Explanation: The curve-subfield correspondence restricts to good subfields and gives a bijection between the points of the matched rational curves.
- **claim-good-functions** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-good-functions)): Let $F/\kappa$ be a finitely generated extension of an algebraically closed field with $\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(F/\kappa)\ge2$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{char}}(\kappa)\ne\ell$. On an integral model of $F/\kappa$, let $q$ be a smooth closed point and let $a,b$ be rational functions regular at $q$, vanishing there, with independent differentials in the cotangent space at $q$. Then $t=a/b$ is a good function. Moreover, for every $f\in F^\times$ there is a good function $t$ such that $ft$ is good. Consequently good functions generate $F$ over $\kappa$, and their classes span $V_F$.
  - Explanation: A quotient of two transverse local parameters at a smooth point is a good function; such functions can be modified by any given element, generate the field and span the multiplicative classes.
- **claim-common-partner** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-common-partner)): For any finite list of good functions $t_1,\ldots,t_m$ of $F/\kappa$ there is a good function $s$ such that $s$ is algebraically independent of each $t_i$ over $\kappa$ and $$t_i+cs\ \hbox{is good for every }i \hbox{ and every }c\in\kappa^\times.$$
  - Explanation: For any finite list of good functions there is a good function algebraically independent of all of them whose nonzero multiples added to each stay good.
- **claim-simultaneous-values** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-simultaneous-values)): For every finite tuple $\mathbf t$ of good functions there are dense open subsets $U\subset X_{\mathbf t}$ and $U'\subset Y_{\mathbf t}$ such that $$\label{eq:field-simultaneous} \left(\prod_i B_{t_i}\right)(U(k))\subset Y_{\mathbf t}(l), \qquad \left(\prod_i B_{t_i}^{-1}\right)(U'(l))\subset X_{\mathbf t}(k).$$
  - Explanation: Point bijections of good subfields, taken coordinatewise, carry a dense open of the relation variety into the matching relation variety in both directions.
- **claim-cofinite-rational** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-cofinite-rational)): A perfect rational function in one variable that is injective on a cofinite subset of $\mathbb P^1(l)$ agrees there with a unique member of $\mathcal H_l$. In characteristic zero, $\mathcal H_l=\mathop{\mathrm{PGL}}_2(l)$; in characteristic $p>0$, every member has a unique expression $$M\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Frob}}^n,\qquad M\in\mathop{\mathrm{PGL}}_2(l),\quad n\in\mathbb Z.$$ In particular, $\mathcal H_l$ embeds in the group of permutations of $\mathbb P^1(l)$ modulo agreement outside finite sets.
  - Explanation: Perfect rational functions in one variable that are injective off a finite set are exactly projective maps composed with powers of Frobenius, with a unique normal form.
- **claim-field-affine** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-field-affine)): Let $t,s$ be algebraically independent good functions such that $z_c=t+cs$ is good for every $c\in k^\times$. For every $c\in k^\times$ there is a perfect rational function $R_c\in l(X,Y)^i$ such that $$\label{eq:field-sum} R_c(x,y)=B_{z_c}\bigl(B_t^{-1}(x)+cB_s^{-1}(y)\bigr) \qquad\text{for general }(x,y)\in l^2.$$ The one-variable slices of these functions give $$\label{eq:field-affine} B_sB_t^{-1}\in\mathcal H_l, \qquad B_t\mathop{\mathrm{Aff}}(k)B_t^{-1}\subset\mathcal H_l.$$ The second inclusion is an embedding of groups.
  - Explanation: The transported addition law is given by perfect rational functions, and conjugating the affine group by the coordinate bijection lands in the group of projective and Frobenius maps, embedded as a group.
- **claim-constant-alignment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-constant-alignment)): The fields $k$ and $l$ have the same characteristic. There is a field isomorphism $\sigma:k\longrightarrow l$ such that, for every good function $t$, there is $H_t\in\mathcal H_l$ with $$\label{eq:field-alignment} B_t=H_t\circ\sigma \qquad\text{outside a finite subset of }\mathbb P^1(k),$$ where $\sigma(\infty)=\infty$.
  - Explanation: The constant fields have equal characteristic and are isomorphic by a single map aligning every coordinate bijection with a projective-Frobenius map outside a finite set.
- **claim-field-isomorphism** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-field-isomorphism)): There is a field isomorphism $$\alpha:K^i\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }L^i,\qquad \alpha\|_k=\sigma,$$ such that $\alpha(t)=u_t$ for every good function $t$. In particular, if $k(t)$ and $l(t')$ are matched good rational subfields, then $\alpha(k(t)^i)=l(t')^i$.
  - Explanation: There is an isomorphism of perfect closures extending the constant-field isomorphism that sends each good function to its aligned image, preserving all algebraic relations.
- **claim-restriction-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-restriction-rank)): Let $v$ be a true prime divisor of $K/k$ and let $E\subset K$ be a curve subfield. The restriction $v\|_E$ is trivial if and only if the image of $D_v\to A_E$ is infinite-dimensional. If $v\|_E$ is nontrivial, this image has dimension at most one.
  - Explanation: A true prime divisor restricts trivially to a curve subfield exactly when its decomposition space restricts with infinite-dimensional image; otherwise the image has dimension at most one.
- **claim-separate-divisors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-separate-divisors)): For distinct true prime divisors $v_1,v_2$ of $K/k$, there is a good rational subfield $E\subset K$ such that $v_1\|_E$ is trivial and $v_2\|_E$ is nontrivial.
  - Explanation: Two distinct true prime divisors can be separated by a good rational subfield on which one restricts trivially and the other does not.
- **claim-global-scalar** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-global-scalar)): Let $\Psi:V_K\to V_K$ be a compatible $\Lambda$-linear automorphism. If $\Psi(V_E)=V_E$ for every good rational subfield $E\subset K$, then $\Psi=a\,\mathrm{id}$ for one $a\in\Lambda^\times$.
  - Explanation: A compatible linear automorphism preserving every good rational subfield's class space acts as one scalar on the whole space.
- **claim-uniqueness-frobenius** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-uniqueness)): Suppose $\beta:K^i\to K^i$ is a field automorphism preserving $k$ whose action on $V_K$ is a scalar. Then $\beta$ is the identity in characteristic zero and an integral power of Frobenius in positive characteristic.
  - Explanation: A field automorphism of the perfect closure preserving the constants and acting by a scalar on the classes is trivial in characteristic zero and a power of Frobenius otherwise.

        "exact_quote": "Assume $s\\geq2$. For any nonzero $h\\in H$, its decomposition space is $$D_v=\\{g\\in A_F:(h,g)\\text{ is alternating}\\}.\n \\label{eq:local-decomposition-recognition}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-009.1"
      },

        }

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-density-input",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F/\\kappa$ be a function field over an algebraically closed field of characteristic different from $\\ell$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-curve-orders",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B/b$ be a function field of transcendence degree one over an algebraically closed field, and let $C/b$ be its smooth projective curve. The family $(\\delta_x)_{x\\in C(b)}$ tends to zero outside finite subsets, and the continuous summation map $$\\label{eq:curve-summation}\n \\prod_{x\\in C(b)}\\Lambda\\longrightarrow A_B,\\qquad\n (a_x)_x\\longmapsto\\sum_x a_x\\delta_x$$ has kernel consisting exactly of the constant families.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-curve-test",
        "exact_quote": "There is an integer $G_X\\ge0$, depending only on the embedded model $X$, with the following property. Given $g_1,\\ldots,g_m\\in L^\\times$ whose classes in $V_L$ are independent, there is a quasi-prime $(d-1)$-divisor $w$ of $L/l$ such that:*\n\n1.  *$lw$ is algebraically closed and algebraic over a finite field of characteristic different from $\\ell$, and $Lw$ is the function field of a smooth projective curve $C_w/lw$ of genus at most $G_X$;*\n\n2.  *the $g_j$ are $w$-units and their residue classes are independent in $V_{Lw}$;*\n\n3.  *for every $j$, $$\\#\\{x\\in C_w(lw):\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_x(\\overline{g_j})\\not\\equiv0\\pmod\\ell\\}\n           =s_X([g_j]).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-009.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $X\\subset\\mathbb P_l^n$ be a normal integral projective variety of dimension $d\\ge2$ over an algebraically closed field $l$ of characteristic different from $\\ell$, and put $L=l(X)$. Let $\\mathcal S$ be an infinite set of prime divisors on $X$ whose degrees are bounded. Suppose that $H\\subset V_L$ is a subspace with the following property: for every $g\\in L^\\times$ with $[g]\\in H$, $$\\label{eq:curve-residue-trivial}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_D(g)=0\\quad\\hbox{and}\\quad\n g|_D\\in l(D)^{\\times\\ell}\n \\qquad\\hbox{for all but finitely many }D\\in\\mathcal S.$$ Then there are a finite extension $M/L$ and a one-variable field $P_0/l$ contained in $M$ such that $M/P_0$ is regular, $$\\label{eq:curve-compositum}\n                               M=LP_0,$$ and the image of $H$ in $V_M$ is contained in the image of $V_{P_0}$. In particular, every $g$ as above has an $\\ell$-th root in $M\\overline{P_0}$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-good-functions",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F/\\kappa$ be a finitely generated extension of an algebraically closed field with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{trdeg}}(F/\\kappa)\\ge2$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{char}}(\\kappa)\\ne\\ell$. On an integral model of $F/\\kappa$, let $q$ be a smooth closed point and let $a,b$ be rational functions regular at $q$, vanishing there, with independent differentials in the cotangent space at $q$. Then $t=a/b$ is a good function. Moreover, for every $f\\in F^\\times$ there is a good function $t$ such that $ft$ is good. Consequently good functions generate $F$ over $\\kappa$, and their classes span $V_F$.",
        "source_id": "PM-009.1"
      },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **compatible isomorphism** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A $\Lambda$-linear isomorphism $\Theta:V_K\to V_L$ is *compatible* if $$(\Theta\otimes\Theta)(R_K)=R_L.$$
- **regular extension** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A field extension is regular if it is separable and its base is relatively algebraically closed.
- **minimized inertia and decomposition spaces** ([source](#sec:local)): The *minimized inertia and decomposition spaces* are $$I_v=U_v^\perp\subset D_v=(U_v^1)^\perp\subset A_F,$$ where perpendiculars refer to evaluation.
- **quasi-divisorial valuation** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): A *quasi-divisorial valuation*, or *quasi-prime divisor*, of $F/\kappa$ is a valuation minimal under coarsening among those with $$vF/v\kappa\cong\mathbb Z,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(Fv/\kappa v)=s-1.$$ A quasi-prime divisor trivial on $\kappa$ is called a *prime divisor*, or a *true prime divisor*. A *quasi-prime $r$-divisor* is a composition of $r$ successive quasi-prime divisors on the resulting residue fields.
- **constant reduction** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A valuation $c$ of a function field $B/b$, with $b$ algebraically closed, is a *constant reduction* if $\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(Bc/bc)=\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(B/b)$.
- **curve subfield** ([source](#sec:curve-fields)): A *curve subfield* of a function field $F/\kappa$ is a subfield $E\subset F$ containing $\kappa$, relatively algebraically closed in $F$, and of transcendence degree one over $\kappa$.
- **good subfield** ([source](#stmt-def-good-subfield)): A curve subfield $E\subset F$ is *good* if it is rational over the constants and every point line in $A_E$ is the nonzero image of the inertia of some true divisor of $F$. A *good function* is an element $t\in F$ generating a good subfield $\kappa(t)$.
- **perfect rational function** ([source](#pm-h-25)): An element of $l(X_1,\ldots,X_r)^i$ will be called a *perfect rational function*.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **r-local-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-local-input) / [proof](#stmt-thm-local-input): Imported alternating-pair valuation theory. -> Valuations from valuative subsets and alternating subspaces.
- **r-abhyankar** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-abhyankar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-abhyankar): Maximal families of rationally independent values and independent residues give algebraically independent elements. -> Valuation inequality with equality cases.
- **r-residue-duality** [claim](#stmt-lem-residue-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-residue-duality): Split exact sequence of unit quotients, dualize into the finite field, check alternation case by case. -> Decomposition modulo inertia is the residue character space.
- **r-local-approximation** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-approximation): Approximation theorem on independent induced valuations. -> Intersection of decomposition spaces equals inertia of the common coarsening.
- **r-local-dimension** [claim](#stmt-prop-local-dimension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-local-dimension): Bound alternating subspaces via the valuative part and the valuation inequality; attain by a coordinate flag. -> Transcendence degree as maximal alternating dimension.
- **r-quasi-recovery** [claim](#stmt-prop-quasi-recovery) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-quasi-recovery): Intersect inertias of two independent flag valuations; converse via approximation and minimality. -> Inertia lines of quasi-divisors characterized by alternating subspaces.
- **r-local-flag-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-flag-uniqueness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-flag-uniqueness): Minimality for one step, convex subgroup argument for flags, then coarsening description. -> Inertia determines the composite valuation.
- **r-flag-matching** [claim](#stmt-cor-flag-matching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-flag-matching): Iterate the recovery of quasi-divisors through residue fields. -> Matched flags and residue character isomorphisms.
- **r-density-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-density-input) / [proof](#stmt-thm-density-input): Imported density theorem for minimized inertia. -> Density of inertia in the residual character set.
- **r-residual-density** [claim](#stmt-lem-residual-density-mod-ell) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-residual-density-mod-ell): Reduce characters modulo the prime and lift inertia lines. -> Residual inertia set contains all c.r. inertia lines.
- **r-residual-valuative** [claim](#stmt-lem-residual-valuative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-residual-valuative): Compactness of the patch space of valuation rings and approximation. -> Closed union of inertias is valuative.
- **r-curve-orders** [claim](#stmt-lem-curve-orders) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-curve-orders): Approximation at points, divisor class group divisibility, torsion of the Jacobian. -> Point-order relation and kernel dimension.
- **r-point-recognition** [claim](#stmt-prop-point-recognition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-point-recognition): Finite-field constants give only point lines; otherwise a constant reduction adds a line violating the kernel condition. -> Intrinsic test for finite-field constants.
- **r-kummer-kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-kummer-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kummer-kernel): Finiteness of the relative algebraic closure and Kummer theory. -> Finite-dimensional kernel of extension maps.
- **r-support-bertini** [claim](#stmt-lem-support-bertini) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-support-bertini): Bertini smoothness and an incidence dimension count for irreducibility. -> Integral smooth hyperplane sections of covers.
- **r-finite-curve-test** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-curve-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-curve-test): Build a curve by general hyperplanes, spread out over a finitely generated ring, specialize and realize by a valuation. -> Testing curve of bounded genus with preserved orders and independence.
- **r-uniform-support** [claim](#stmt-prop-uniform-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-uniform-support): Transfer five anchor classes to a residual curve and apply Riemann–Hurwitz to a pencil morphism. -> Uniform bound on supports of image pencil classes.
- **r-constant-descent** [claim](#stmt-lem-constant-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-constant-descent): Galois cocycle of roots of unity and Hilbert's Theorem 90. -> Classes with roots over algebraic constants come from the base.
- **r-curve-intersection** [claim](#stmt-lem-curve-intersection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-curve-intersection): Compositum is the function field of a product of curves; compare kernels of point orders. -> Finite intersections of class spaces of distinct curve subfields.
- **r-curve-incidence** [claim](#stmt-lem-curve-incidence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-curve-incidence): Bounded parameter space, incidence family over a curve, splitting of Kummer covers, descent from constants. -> One-variable field containing the tested classes after a finite extension.
- **r-curve-matching** [claim](#stmt-thm-curve-matching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-curve-matching): Infinite bounded-degree support, residue powers along divisors, incidence descent to a curve subfield, apply the inverse map. -> Bijection of curve subfields.
- **r-true-divisors** [claim](#stmt-prop-true-divisors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-true-divisors): Finite-dimensional kernel test for trivial valuations; infinitely many principal units otherwise. -> Matching of true prime divisors.
- **r-good-matching** [claim](#stmt-prop-good-matching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-good-matching): Point lines fill the summation kernel condition; genus zero recognized by vanishing common kernel. -> Bijection of good subfields and their points.
- **r-good-functions** [claim](#stmt-lem-good-functions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-good-functions): Blow up a smooth point and read off the exceptional divisor valuation. -> Supply of good functions generating the field.
- **r-common-partner** [claim](#stmt-lem-common-partner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-common-partner): Choose transverse parameters at a point where all given functions are regular. -> Independent good partner function.
- **r-simultaneous-values** [claim](#stmt-lem-simultaneous-values) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-simultaneous-values): Exceptional divisor of a blowup detects all coordinates at once; specialize relations. -> Relation varieties matched on dense opens.
- **r-cofinite-rational** [claim](#stmt-lem-cofinite-rational) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cofinite-rational): Degree-one argument after removing Frobenius powers. -> Normal form of cofinitely injective perfect rational functions.
- **r-field-affine** [claim](#stmt-lem-field-affine) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-field-affine): Dimension count on the relation variety, slices and cofinite normal form. -> Transported addition is perfect rational and affine group lands in the projective-Frobenius group.
- **r-constant-alignment** [claim](#stmt-prop-constant-alignment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-constant-alignment): Translation subgroup yields a field embedding, shown onto by an identity of rational functions. -> Constant-field isomorphism aligning all coordinate bijections.
- **r-field-isomorphism** [claim](#stmt-thm-field-isomorphism) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-field-isomorphism): Match relation varieties, extend to quotients and to perfect closures, show surjectivity. -> Isomorphism of perfect closures.
- **r-restriction-rank** [claim](#stmt-lem-restriction-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-restriction-rank): Kernel finiteness for trivial restriction; cyclic value group otherwise. -> Rank dichotomy for restrictions of decomposition spaces.
- **r-separate-divisors** [claim](#stmt-lem-separate-divisors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separate-divisors): Chinese remainder construction of a quotient of two functions on a common model. -> Good subfield separating two divisors.
- **r-global-scalar** [claim](#stmt-prop-global-scalar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-global-scalar): Identity permutation of divisors, scalar on each rational subfield, compare through a blown-up point. -> One global scalar.
- **r-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-prop-uniqueness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-uniqueness): Scalar automorphisms preserve curve subfields, fix points of the line, hence are Frobenius powers. -> Frobenius ambiguity only.
- **r-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-uniqueness-2): Combine equality of dimensions, the field isomorphism, the global scalar and uniqueness up to Frobenius. -> Bijectivity of the canonical map.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-main-bijection**: For finitely generated extensions of algebraically closed fields with constants of characteristic different from the prime and relative transcendence degree at least two, the canonical map from field isomorphisms of perfect closures (modulo Frobenius powers in positive characteristic) to compatible isomorphisms of the degree-one groups modulo scalars is bijective.
- **inv-max-alternating-dimension**: The maximum dimension of an alternating subspace of the character space of a function field equals its transcendence degree over the constants.
- **inv-scalar-automorphism-frobenius**: A field automorphism of the perfect closure that preserves the constants and acts as a scalar on the degree-one group is the identity in characteristic zero and an integral power of Frobenius in positive characteristic.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-curves**: Applying the reconstruction to function fields of curves, i.e. relative transcendence degree one. -> The argument needs relative transcendence degree at least two; no reconstruction is claimed for curves.
- **anti-char-equals-ell**: Using the result with the working prime equal to the characteristic of the constants. -> The theorem is stated only when the characteristics of the constants differ from the prime.
- **anti-non-closed-constants**: Applying the theorem to function fields whose constant field is not algebraically closed. -> The statement covers only finitely generated extensions of algebraically closed fields; nothing is asserted otherwise.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existence and reconstruction theorems about infinite objects (function fields over arbitrary algebraically closed fields, valuations, compact character spaces, curve subfields). The proofs use choices of valuations, closures, Hilbert schemes and specialization; no finite procedure with explicit steps is stated, so no finite computation can be executed as a faithful oracle.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main result is an existence and uniqueness statement for field isomorphisms. It gives no finite procedure that code could run, so there is nothing to implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The objects are abstract fields, valuations and character spaces over algebraically closed constants. They have no discrete state, dynamics or rules to simulate. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source states no checker, solver or utility. Its quantitative content is a genus bound that depends on a fixed embedded model, with no procedure to compute it. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Proofs use existence arguments: maximal alternating subspaces, general hyperplanes, valuation prolongation and generic choices. None yields an effective procedure or a complexity bound. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The point-order families on a curve are infinite, topologised objects. The source describes them for a proof and offers no representation with operations or costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the source concerns a bounded-precision or parallel computation. Its generic constructions are free of any cardinality restriction on the constants. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite fields appear only as a residue-constant criterion on recognised curves. The results give no hardness assumption, primitive or protocol, and the paper is within pure anabelian reconstruction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no data, loss, learning or statistical model. The only generation statement is purely algebraic: good functions generate the field and span the mod-ell classes. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only extremal-type statement is a uniform finiteness bound on supports in a pencil. It is not an objective, feasible set or optimisation problem. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no processes, messages or consensus. The word detection appears only for simultaneous values of finitely many functions at one valuation, a purely geometric device. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is an internal contribution to birational anabelian geometry. It is not tied to a fresh non-listed engineering domain, and it never lifts to a pro-ell statement. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-trdeg-and-char-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The reconstruction theorem is stated only for finitely generated extensions of algebraically closed fields, with constants of characteristic different from the prime and relative transcendence degree at least two on both sides. Blocked use: ['Applying the bijection to function fields of curves', 'Applying it when the characteristic of the constants equals the working prime', 'Applying it to function fields over constants that are not algebraically closed']
- **L-frobenius-ambiguity** (formal_scope): The reconstructed field isomorphism lives on perfect closures and is determined only up to postcomposition with powers of Frobenius in positive characteristic. Blocked use: ['Claiming an isomorphism of the original imperfect fields', 'Claiming a unique field isomorphism in positive characteristic']
- **L-global-scalar-ambiguity** (formal_scope): Compatible isomorphisms are recovered only modulo multiplication by a nonzero scalar of the prime field, which acts on the set of compatible isomorphisms and changes the degree-two map by a square. Blocked use: ['Treating a compatible isomorphism as determining a unique field isomorphism without quotienting by scalars']
- **L-compatibility-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The scalar-uniqueness step applies to compatible linear automorphisms only, not to arbitrary linear automorphisms of the degree-one group. Blocked use: ['Applying the global scalar conclusion to a linear map that does not preserve the Steinberg relations']
- **L-kummer-roots-of-unity** (hypothesis): Finite-dimensionality of the kernel of the extension map is proved only when the base has characteristic different from the prime and contains the prime roots of unity; the argument uses Kummer theory. Blocked use: ['Using the kernel bound in residue fields of characteristic equal to the prime', 'Using it over a base lacking the roots of unity']
- **L-density-input-valuation-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The imported density theorem needs a valuation whose value group has no nonzero divisible convex subgroup; the residual density lemma depends on this being verified for the terminal valuation. Blocked use: ['Invoking the density input for valuations with a nonzero divisible convex subgroup in the value group']
- **L-nonconstructive-specialization** (nonconstructive): The testing-curve construction spreads data out over a finitely generated ring, picks a closed point and a valuation dominating it; these are existence choices, so no algorithm or explicit genus value follows. Blocked use: ['Reading the genus bound as a computable constant', 'Implementing the test curve as an effective procedure']
- **L-no-pro-ell-lift** (formal_scope): The mod-prime isomorphism is never lifted to a pro-prime isomorphism; density input is used for individual fields only. Blocked use: ['Citing the result as a pro-ell Galois reconstruction theorem']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove a mod-<i>ℓ</i> Bogomolov–Pop reconstruction theorem for function fields of transcendence degree at least two over arbitrary algebraically closed fields of characteristic different from <i>ℓ</i>. The groups $`K^{\mathrm M}_1/\ell`$ and $`K^{\mathrm M}_2/\ell`$, together with their full bilinear product, determine the perfect closure and its constant field. Every compatible isomorphism of these data is induced by a field isomorphism up to a single scalar in $`\mathbb F_\ell^\times`$, with only Frobenius ambiguity in the field isomorphism in positive characteristic.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Birational anabelian geometry asks how much of a function field can be recovered from its Galois-theoretic invariants. In the Bogomolov program, the field has algebraically closed constants, and the relevant invariants are much smaller than the full absolute Galois group. The present paper proves the reconstruction assertion for the first two Milnor $K$-groups modulo a single prime, with their multiplication. The result includes the isomorphism statement: it identifies every compatible linear isomorphism, as well as the ambiguity in the field isomorphism that induces it.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The reconstruction theorem

Fix a prime $\ell$ and put $\Lambda=\mathbb F_\ell$. For a field $F$ of characteristic different from $\ell$, set $$V_F=F^\times/(F^\times)^\ell,
 \qquad
 R_F=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}_\Lambda\{[x]\otimes[1-x]:x\in F\setminus\{0,1\}\}.$$ We write $V_F$ additively, so $[xy]=[x]+[y]$. Let $$W_F=(V_F\otimes_\Lambda V_F)/R_F,
 \qquad m_F:V_F\otimes_\Lambda V_F\longrightarrow W_F.$$ Thus $V_F=K^{\mathrm M}_1(F)/\ell$ and $W_F=K^{\mathrm M}_2(F)/\ell$, with $m_F$ the Milnor product. A $\Lambda$-linear isomorphism $\Theta:V_K\to V_L$ is *compatible* if $$(\Theta\otimes\Theta)(R_K)=R_L.$$ Equivalently, there is a unique linear isomorphism $\Theta_2:W_K\to W_L$ intertwining the products. Write $\mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}_{\mathrm M}(V_K,V_L)$ for the set of compatible isomorphisms. Multiplication by $a\in\Lambda^\times$ acts on this set; the induced degree-two map is then multiplied by $a^2$.

Let $K/k$ and $L/l$ be finitely generated extensions of algebraically closed fields. Write $F^i$ for the perfect closure of a field $F$: it equals $F$ in characteristic zero and $\bigcup_{r\ge0}F^{1/p^r}$ in characteristic $p>0$. Denote by $\mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}^i(K,L)$ the field isomorphisms $\alpha:K^i\to L^i$ satisfying $\alpha(k)=l$. In positive characteristic, identify two such isomorphisms if they differ by postcomposition with $\mathop{\mathrm{Frob}}_L^n$, $n\in\mathbb Z$, where $\mathop{\mathrm{Frob}}_L(z)=z^p$. Denote the resulting set by $\mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}^i_{\mathrm F}(K,L)$; in characteristic zero take no quotient, and if the characteristics differ this set is empty.

<a id="eq:main-map"></a>
Purely inseparable extension induces canonical isomorphisms on $V$ and $W$. Indeed, for an extension of exponent at most $r$, inclusion and the $p^r$-power homomorphism compose to the $p^r$-power map on each field. On Milnor groups in degrees one and two this composition is multiplication by $p^r$ and $p^{2r}$, respectively, both invertible modulo $\ell$. Passing to the union proves the assertion for perfect closures. Thus $\alpha$ induces a compatible map $\alpha_1:V_K\to V_L$, and postcomposition by $\mathop{\mathrm{Frob}}_L^n$ multiplies it by $p^n$ in $\Lambda^\times$. There is consequently a canonical map $$\label{eq:main-map}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}^i_{\mathrm F}(K,L)\longrightarrow
 \mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}_{\mathrm M}(V_K,V_L)/\Lambda^\times.$$

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $\ell$ be any prime. Let $K/k$ and $L/l$ be finitely generated extensions of arbitrary algebraically closed fields, with $\mathop{\mathrm{char}}k,\mathop{\mathrm{char}}l\ne\ell$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(K/k),\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(L/l)\ge2$. Then <a href="#eq:main-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main-map">[eq:main-map]</a> is bijective. In particular, a compatible isomorphism $V_K\to V_L$ forces equality of the characteristics and of the relative transcendence degrees.*

</div>

Only the two vector spaces and their bilinear product enter this statement. The proof recovers the valuations and rational-subfield images needed for reconstruction from this datum. The scalar is one global element of $\mathbb F_\ell^\times$; at $\ell=2$ this ambiguity is trivial.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Historical context

Bogomolov proposed reconstructing higher-dimensional function fields over algebraically closed constants from the pro-$\ell$ quotient in which commutators are central <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Bogomolov1991](#ref-Bogomolov1991)]. Bogomolov and Tschinkel proved reconstruction for surfaces over algebraic closures of finite fields <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[BT2008](#ref-BT2008)], and subsequently for higher-dimensional function fields over those constants <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[BT2011](#ref-BT2011)]. Pop independently completed reconstruction in this constant-field setting <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[PopBI2012](#ref-PopBI2012)]. These results established that a small part of the Galois group can retain the geometry needed to recover a field. Over more general algebraically closed constants, Pop also obtained reconstruction from pro-$\ell$ data endowed with divisorial inertia, in relative transcendence degree greater than two <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Pop2018](#ref-Pop2018), Theorem 1.1].

The finite-coefficient problem requires additional control of the global reconstruction. Topaz proved a Milnor-theoretic isomorphism theorem in relative transcendence degree at least five when the degree-one and degree-two mod-$\ell$ groups and their product are supplied together with all rational subgroups <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Topaz2016](#ref-Topaz2016), Theorem B]. Those subgroups are the images of the multiplicative groups of relatively algebraically closed rational one-variable subfields. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> obtains the required rational subfields from the product itself, and its point-value argument applies in every relative dimension at least two.

Algebraic dependence has provided another route from multiplicative invariants to field structure. Bogomolov and Tschinkel used Milnor $K$-theory modulo infinitely divisible elements in characteristic zero <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[BT2009](#ref-BT2009)]. Cadoret and Pirutka reconstructed regular function fields over perfect constants from the multiplicative quotient by constants together with algebraic dependence, and derived applications to integral Milnor $K$-theory <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[CadoretPirutka2021](#ref-CadoretPirutka2021)]. Topaz later proved reconstruction from rational Milnor $K$-theory in absolute transcendence degree at least five <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Topaz2022](#ref-Topaz2022)]. These results clarify the role of algebraic dependence, while using invariants different from the single-prime datum considered here.

Our local inputs are the alternating-pair valuation theory recorded in <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Topaz2020](#ref-Topaz2020), Section 2] and Pop’s density theorem for minimized inertia <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Pop2015](#ref-Pop2015)]. We state the precise forms used below. The bounded-support and incidence method is adapted from <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[InternalBP](#ref-InternalBP), Sections 4–6]. The finite-coefficient bounded-support and incidence arguments are proved here. The support estimate also has an antecedent in the bounded-genus curve sections and Hurwitz argument of Bogomolov and Tschinkel <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BT2011](#ref-BT2011), Section 6, Proposition 6.1]. The proof uses no pro-$\ell$ field-reconstruction theorem as a premise.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The proof and its main ingredients

The argument begins with a compatible map $\Theta$ and its dual on the compact character spaces $A_F=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(F^\times,\Lambda)$. Two characters $f,g$ form an *alternating pair* when $f(x)g(1-x)=f(1-x)g(x)$ for every $x\ne0,1$. This condition is visible in the Milnor product and is therefore preserved by $\Theta$. Established local theory turns alternating subspaces into valuations. Section <a href="#sec:local" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:local">2</a> uses it to recover the transcendence degree and the inertia and decomposition spaces of quasi-divisorial valuations, and the character spaces of their iterated residue fields. These valuations may act nontrivially on the constants; their residue constants can therefore have positive characteristic even when the original field has characteristic zero.

The next task is to recover curve subfields: relatively algebraically closed intermediate fields $k\subset E\subset K$ of transcendence degree one over $k$. Individual mod-$\ell$ classes record only orders modulo $\ell$, so a divisor can disappear when its multiplicity is divisible by $\ell$. We control this loss by passing to residual curves. Section <a href="#sec:residual-curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:residual-curves">3</a> uses Pop’s density theorem to recognize residual curves whose constants are algebraic closures of finite fields, together with all their point order lines. Section <a href="#sec:support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:support">4</a> then constructs a residual curve of bounded genus for any finite independent list of classes in $V_L$. It preserves independence and realizes each support degree on a fixed projective model of $L$ as the number of nonzero point orders on the residual curve. For each nonconstant $t\in K$, applying Riemann–Hurwitz to five fixed members of its pencil bounds the total degree of the divisor support of every class $\Theta([t-a])$, $a\in k$, on that model.

For a curve subfield $E$, choose $t\in E\setminus k$. The supporting prime divisors of the classes $\Theta([t-a])$ form an infinite set of bounded degree, hence lie in a finite-type family. The valuation correspondence shows that each class in $\Theta(V_E)$ has an $\ell$-th-power residue along all but finitely many of these divisors. Section <a href="#sec:curve-fields" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:curve-fields">5</a> studies the incidence variety of pairs consisting of a point and a divisor containing it. Restricting its parameter space to a curve produces a finite extension of $L$ containing the curve’s function field. After passage to this extension, the classes come from that one-variable parameter field. The argument then descends them to a curve subfield $P\subset L$ and proves $\Theta(V_E)=V_P$.

Two features are useful specifically for finite coefficients. One parameter curve works for every class. For each class, a finite change of parameter space allows normality to extend a generic $\ell$-th root of a representative across the smooth locus where that representative is a unit. This avoids any restriction on the cardinality of the constants. Moreover, the incidence field is the compositum of $L$ and the parameter field. This identity permits exact descent, retaining information that a norm could annihilate modulo $\ell$.

Once curve subfields correspond, Section <a href="#sec:good-functions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:good-functions">6</a> recognizes true divisors, which are trivial on the constants, and a sufficiently large family of rational subfields on which every point is detected. A quotient of two local parameters at a smooth point supplies such a subfield. These quotients generate the function field, and the construction works already in dimension two. Matching their point-order lines gives bijections between the corresponding projective lines of points.

Section <a href="#sec:field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:field">7</a> recovers field operations from simultaneous values of these rational functions. A blowup at a smooth point detects all coordinates of a finite tuple at once. Additive triples yield rational expressions, allowing $p$-power roots in characteristic $p$, for the transported addition law. Their one-variable slices align all point bijections, outside finite sets, with a single isomorphism of constant fields. An identity of rational functions removes the apparent freedom caused by finite exceptional sets in those slices. The resulting correspondence preserves every algebraic relation among the chosen generators and hence gives an isomorphism of perfect fields.

Finally, Section <a href="#sec:compatibility" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:compatibility">8</a> proves that this isomorphism induces the original map up to one scalar, and that scalar action of a field automorphism forces it to be a Frobenius power. Throughout, the pro-$\ell$ density input concerns individual fields. The given mod-$\ell$ isomorphism is never lifted to a pro-$\ell$ isomorphism.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Conventions

A function field $F/\kappa$ means a finitely generated field extension. Its constants $\kappa$ will be algebraically closed. A curve is a geometrically integral variety of dimension one, and its function field has a unique smooth projective model over algebraically closed constants. A field extension is regular if it is separable and its base is relatively algebraically closed. Points of varieties over an algebraically closed field mean rational points unless a scheme point is specified. The phrase “for general points” means on some dense Zariski open subset. We continue to use $V_F=F^\times/(F^\times)^\ell$ for residue fields of characteristic $\ell$, as a multiplicative quotient, without a Galois-cohomological interpretation.

<a id="sec:local"></a>
### Alternating characters and quasi-divisorial valuations

We first recover valuations from the degree-two relations. The local theory is most naturally expressed on the dual of the multiplicative group. Its multiplicative formulation also applies to residue fields of characteristic $\ell$, which will occur in the argument.

For any field $F$, put $$A_F=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(F^\times,\Lambda)
     =\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_\Lambda(V_F,\Lambda),\qquad \Lambda=\mathbb F_\ell.$$ Here the definition of $V_F$ is used in every characteristic. Give $A_F$ the topology of pointwise convergence, with $\Lambda$ discrete. Choosing a basis of $V_F$ identifies $A_F$ with a product of copies of $\Lambda$. In particular it is compact, and evaluation identifies $V_F$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{cont}}(A_F,\Lambda)$: a continuous linear form on that product depends on only finitely many coordinates.

<a id="eq:local-alternating"></a>
A pair $f,g\in A_F$ is *alternating* if $$f(x)g(1-x)=f(1-x)g(x)\qquad(x\in F\setminus\{0,1\}).
 \label{eq:local-alternating}$$ A subset is alternating if every pair of its elements is alternating. The determinant functional $$[x]\otimes[y]\longmapsto f(x)g(y)-f(y)g(x)$$ annihilates $R_F$ exactly when $f,g$ are alternating. Thus the isomorphism $\Theta$ in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> induces a continuous linear isomorphism $$\Phi=\Theta^*:A_L\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }A_K$$ that preserves alternating pairs in both directions.

We consider valuations up to equivalence and write $v\leq w$ when $v$ is a coarsening of $w$. Write $U_v$ and $U_v^1$ for its units and principal units, $Fv$ for its residue field, and $vF$ for its value group. The *minimized inertia and decomposition spaces* are $$I_v=U_v^\perp\subset D_v=(U_v^1)^\perp\subset A_F,$$ where perpendiculars refer to evaluation. A subset of $A_F$ is *valuative* if it is contained in some $I_v$. Restriction to units gives a continuous map $$\rho_v:D_v\longrightarrow A_{Fv}$$ with kernel $I_v$. Surjectivity will be established for the valuations we use below.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### The established local input

The following form of alternating-pair valuation theory collects <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Topaz2020](#ref-Topaz2020), Fact 2.1, Lemma 2.5, Theorem 2.6, and Corollary 2.7]. We state it for finite coefficients; the field in this statement need not have characteristic different from $\ell$.

<a id="stmt-thm-local-input"></a>
<div id="thm:local-input" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Alternating-pair valuation theory). *Let $F$ be a field and set $A_F^{\pm}=\{f\in A_F:f(-1)=0\}$.*

1.  *Every valuative subset $\Sigma\subset A_F$ has a unique coarsest valuation $v_\Sigma$ with $\Sigma\subset I_{v_\Sigma}$. In particular, $v_\Sigma$ coarsens every valuation whose inertia contains $\Sigma$. For such a valuation $w$, this coarsening is obtained by quotienting $wF$ by its largest convex subgroup contained in $w(\bigcap_{f\in\Sigma}\ker f)$.*

2.  *If $S\subset A_F^{\pm}$ is an alternating linear subspace, its valuative elements form a valuative subspace $S_0$ of codimension at most one. Moreover $S\subset D_{v_{S_0}}$.*

3.  *If $\Sigma\subset A_F^{\pm}$ is valuative and $g\in A_F^{\pm}$ is alternating with every element of $\Sigma$, then $g\in D_{v_\Sigma}$.*

</div>

The coefficient convention in <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Topaz2020](#ref-Topaz2020), Section 2.1] explicitly allows $\mathbb Z/\ell$; its fraction field is then $\mathbb F_\ell$, so its character space is exactly $A_F$. The case $\ell=2$ is included in the local theorem. For the fields over algebraically closed constants considered here, every character kills the constants and hence $-1$, so $A_F^{\pm}=A_F$.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Value groups and residue characters

For the remainder of this section, let $F/\kappa$ be a finitely generated extension of an algebraically closed field, and put $s=\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(F/\kappa)$. No restriction is imposed on its characteristic. We first record the valuation facts that turn the local theorem into an intrinsic description of codimension-one valuations.

<a id="stmt-lem-local-abhyankar"></a>
<div id="lem:local-abhyankar" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:local-abhyankar"></a>
**Lemma 3**. *For every valuation $v$ of $F$, the field $\kappa v$ is algebraically closed, the group $vF/v\kappa$ is torsion free, and $$\mathop{\mathrm{rrank}}(vF/v\kappa)+\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(Fv/\kappa v)\leq s.
 \label{eq:local-abhyankar}$$ Here $\mathop{\mathrm{rrank}}(G)=\dim_\mathbb Q(G\otimes_\mathbb Z\mathbb Q)$. If equality holds, then $Fv/\kappa v$ is finitely generated and $vF/v\kappa$ is a finitely generated free abelian group. Equality passes to every coarsening and to the induced residue valuation. Conversely, equality for a valuation and an induced residue valuation gives equality for their composition.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-local-abhyankar"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-local-abhyankar</summary>

*Proof.* The value group of an algebraically closed field is divisible, and its residue field is algebraically closed. If $n\gamma\in v\kappa$ for $\gamma\in vF$, choose $\delta\in v\kappa$ with $n\delta=n\gamma$. Ordered abelian groups are torsion free, so $\gamma=\delta$. This proves torsion-freeness of the relative group.

Choose elements $x_1,\ldots,x_a\in F^\times$ whose values are rationally independent modulo $v\kappa$, and units $y_1,\ldots,y_b$ whose residues are algebraically independent over $\kappa v$. These elements are algebraically independent over $\kappa$. Indeed, in a putative polynomial relation, distinct monomials in the $x_i$ have distinct values modulo $v\kappa$. Within a coefficient polynomial in the $y_j$, its terms of least value cannot cancel, by algebraic independence of their residues after rescaling by a constant. The nonzero summands obtained by grouping according to monomials in the $x_i$ have distinct values, so their sum cannot vanish. This proves <a href="#eq:local-abhyankar" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-abhyankar">[eq:local-abhyankar]</a> by taking maximal such families.

Suppose equality holds and choose these families with $a+b=s$. Then $F$ is finite over $F'=\kappa(x_1,\ldots,x_a,y_1,\ldots,y_b)$. The same least-value calculation gives $$vF'/v\kappa\cong\mathbb Z^a,\qquad
 F'v=\kappa v(\bar y_1,\ldots,\bar y_b).$$ The fundamental inequality for the finite extension $F/F'$ shows that $[vF:vF']$ and $[Fv:F'v]$ are finite. Thus the residue extension is finitely generated, while the relative value group is finitely generated and torsion free, hence free.

For the assertion about composition, write $v=w\circ u$, meaning that $u$ is a coarsening and $w$ is the induced valuation on $Fu$. The value groups fit into the exact sequence $$0\longrightarrow w(Fu)/w(\kappa u)
 \longrightarrow vF/v\kappa
 \longrightarrow uF/u\kappa\longrightarrow0.$$ Apply <a href="#eq:local-abhyankar" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-abhyankar">[eq:local-abhyankar]</a> first to $u$, and then to $w$ on $Fu/\kappa u$. The sum of the two inequalities is precisely the inequality for $v$. Consequently equality for $v$ forces equality in both, and equality in both forces equality for $v$. This use of the inequality on $Fu/\kappa u$ is legitimate even before finite generation is known: the algebraic-independence argument proving it applies to arbitrary field extensions of finite transcendence degree. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-auto-definition-1"></a>
<div class="definition">

**Definition 4**. A *quasi-divisorial valuation*, or *quasi-prime divisor*, of $F/\kappa$ is a valuation minimal under coarsening among those with $$vF/v\kappa\cong\mathbb Z,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(Fv/\kappa v)=s-1.$$ A quasi-prime divisor trivial on $\kappa$ is called a *prime divisor*, or a *true prime divisor*. A *quasi-prime $r$-divisor* is a composition of $r$ successive quasi-prime divisors on the resulting residue fields.

</div>

Lemma <a href="#lem:local-abhyankar" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:local-abhyankar">3</a> shows that for a quasi-prime $r$-divisor $v$, the residue field is a function field of transcendence degree $s-r$ over $\kappa v$, and <a id="eq:local-flag-rank"></a>$$vF/v\kappa\cong\mathbb Z^r.
 \label{eq:local-flag-rank}$$ The isomorphism concerns abstract abelian groups; no ordering of $\mathbb Z^r$ is specified.

<a id="stmt-lem-residue-duality"></a>
<div id="lem:residue-duality" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:local-composition"></a>
**Lemma 5**. *Suppose $vF/v\kappa$ is free abelian of finite rank. Then restriction to units induces a canonical topological isomorphism <a id="eq:local-residue-duality"></a>$$D_v/I_v\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }A_{Fv}.
 \label{eq:local-residue-duality}$$ Two elements of $D_v$ are alternating if and only if their images in $A_{Fv}$ are alternating. If $w$ is a valuation of $Fv$, then $$I_{w\circ v}=\rho_v^{-1}(I_w),\qquad
 D_{w\circ v}=\rho_v^{-1}(D_w),
 \label{eq:local-composition}$$ where the inverse images are taken inside $D_v$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-residue-duality"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-residue-duality</summary>

*Proof.* There is an exact sequence of abelian groups $$1\longrightarrow (Fv)^\times/(\kappa v)^\times
 \longrightarrow F^\times/(\kappa^\times U_v^1)
 \longrightarrow vF/v\kappa\longrightarrow0.$$ For the kernel assertion, rescale by a constant an element whose value lies in $v\kappa$, and then take its residue. The last group is free, so the sequence splits as a sequence of abstract groups. Dualizing into $\Lambda$ is consequently exact. Characters kill the algebraically closed constants on both sides, giving the asserted surjection $\rho_v$ with kernel $I_v$. Its induced bijection is a homeomorphism because its source is compact and its target Hausdorff.

For alternation, if $v(x)>0$, then $1-x\in U_v^1$, and the identity <a href="#eq:local-alternating" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-alternating">[eq:local-alternating]</a> holds for any pair in $D_v$. The case $v(1-x)>0$ is the same. If $v(x)<0$, then $(1-x)/(-x)\in U_v^1$, so every character in $D_v$ takes the same value on $x$ and $1-x$. The remaining case is $v(x)=v(1-x)=0$, when the identity is exactly its residue-field counterpart. Conversely, lift any residue element different from zero and one to test the residue identity.

Finally $U_v^1$ is contained in both $U_{w\circ v}$ and $U_{w\circ v}^1$. Under reduction of $v$-units, these two groups map respectively onto $U_w$ and $U_w^1$. Their annihilators therefore give <a href="#eq:local-composition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-composition">[eq:local-composition]</a>. ◻

</details>

We will also use the following consequence of valuation approximation. It explains why intersections of decomposition spaces yield inertia.

<a id="stmt-lem-local-approximation"></a>
<div id="lem:local-approximation" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6**. *Let $v_1,v_2$ be incomparable valuations of a field, and let $u$ be their finest common coarsening. Then $$U_{v_1}^1U_{v_2}^1=U_u,
 \qquad D_{v_1}\cap D_{v_2}=I_u.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-local-approximation"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-local-approximation</summary>

*Proof.* The induced valuations $w_1,w_2$ on the residue field $Fu$ are independent. For $a\in(Fu)^\times$, the approximation theorem gives $b\in(Fu)^\times$ with $$w_1(b-1)>0,\qquad w_2(b-a)>w_2(a).$$ Thus $b\in U_{w_1}^1$ and $a/b\in U_{w_2}^1$, proving $U_{w_1}^1U_{w_2}^1=(Fu)^\times$. Reduction maps $U_{v_i}^1$ onto $U_{w_i}^1$, and both groups $U_{v_i}^1$ contain $U_u^1$. Lifting the product identity gives the first equality; taking annihilators gives the second. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Recovering dimension and quasi-divisors

The next argument follows the alternating-space method of <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Topaz2020](#ref-Topaz2020), Fact 3.2 and Theorem 3.3]. We give the details so that the argument remains available in characteristic $\ell$.

<a id="stmt-prop-local-dimension"></a>
<div id="prop:local-dimension" class="proposition">

**Proposition 7**. *The maximum dimension of an alternating subspace of $A_F$ is $s$. If $S\subset A_F$ is alternating of dimension $s$, and $v$ is the valuation associated to its valuative part $S_0$, then $S_0=I_v$ and equality holds in <a href="#eq:local-abhyankar" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-abhyankar">[eq:local-abhyankar]</a> for $v$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-local-dimension"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-local-dimension</summary>

*Proof.* For any torsion-free abelian group $G$ of finite rational rank, $$\dim_\Lambda(G/\ell G)\leq\mathop{\mathrm{rrank}}(G).$$ Indeed, representatives of linearly independent classes modulo $\ell$ are rationally independent: an integral relation can be divided by the greatest common divisor of its coefficients, using torsion-freeness, and the resulting relation has a coefficient nonzero modulo $\ell$. Since characters of $vF$ kill its divisible subgroup $v\kappa$, this proves $$\dim I_v\leq\mathop{\mathrm{rrank}}(vF/v\kappa).$$

Now let $S$ be alternating. By Theorem <a href="#thm:local-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:local-input">2</a>, its valuative part $S_0$ is a subspace of codimension at most one and, for its associated valuation $v$, $S_0\subset I_v$ and $S\subset D_v$. Moreover $S\cap I_v=S_0$, since every element of $I_v$ is valuative. If $S=S_0$, then $$\dim S\leq\dim I_v\leq\mathop{\mathrm{rrank}}(vF/v\kappa)\leq s.$$ If $S\ne S_0$, an element of $S\setminus S_0$ has nonzero image under $\rho_v$, whose kernel is always $I_v$. Thus $A_{Fv}\ne0$. Since $\kappa v$ is algebraically closed, this implies $\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(Fv/\kappa v)\geq1$, and hence $$\dim S=\dim S_0+1
 \leq\mathop{\mathrm{rrank}}(vF/v\kappa)+\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(Fv/\kappa v)\leq s.$$ If $\dim S=s$, equality throughout the applicable chain gives $S_0=I_v$ and equality in <a href="#eq:local-abhyankar" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-abhyankar">[eq:local-abhyankar]</a>.

To attain the bound, choose a transcendence basis of $F/\kappa$, take a full coordinate flag on its rational function field, and prolong the associated valuation to $F$. It is trivial on $\kappa$, and its value group is a finite-index extension of $\mathbb Z^s$, hence is abstractly $\mathbb Z^s$. Its inertia has dimension $s$ and is alternating by Lemma <a href="#lem:residue-duality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:residue-duality">5</a>. For $s=0$, the field is $\kappa$, its character space is zero, and the assertion has the same interpretation. ◻

</details>

We can now identify the one-dimensional inertia spaces solely through maximal alternating subspaces. Once inertia is identified, alternation with it identifies decomposition as well.

<a id="stmt-prop-quasi-recovery"></a>
<div id="prop:quasi-recovery" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:local-inertia-intersection"></a>
**Proposition 8**. *Assume $s\geq2$. A line $H\subset A_F$ is the inertia of a quasi-divisorial valuation if and only if $$H=S_1\cap S_2
 \quad\text{for alternating subspaces }S_1,S_2\subset A_F
 \text{ of dimension }s.
 \label{eq:local-inertia-intersection}$$ The quasi-divisorial valuation $v$ with $I_v=H$ is unique. For any nonzero $h\in H$, its decomposition space is <a id="eq:local-decomposition-recognition"></a>$$D_v=\{g\in A_F:(h,g)\text{ is alternating}\}.
 \label{eq:local-decomposition-recognition}$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-quasi-recovery"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-quasi-recovery</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose first that $v$ is quasi-divisorial. On $Fv/\kappa v$ choose two independent full discrete flag valuations $w_1,w_2$, trivial on $\kappa v$. To obtain them, start with coordinate flags on a rational subfield of transcendence degree $s-1$, with distinct first prime divisors, and prolong to the finite extension $Fv$. Independence survives prolongation. Indeed, a valuation on a finite field extension that restricts trivially to the smaller field is trivial, since its value group is then finite and hence zero. Thus a common nontrivial coarsening of $w_1,w_2$ would restrict to a common nontrivial coarsening of the original independent flag valuations, which is impossible.

The inertia spaces $I_{w_1},I_{w_2}$ have zero intersection by approximation. Set $v_i=w_i\circ v$ and $S_i=I_{v_i}$. Each $v_i$ has relative value group $\mathbb Z^s$, so its inertia is alternating of dimension $s$. By <a href="#eq:local-composition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-composition">[eq:local-composition]</a>, the intersection $S_1\cap S_2$ is $I_v$.

Conversely, suppose <a href="#eq:local-inertia-intersection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-inertia-intersection">[eq:local-inertia-intersection]</a> holds. Let $S_{i,0}$ be the valuative part of $S_i$, with associated valuation $v_i$. Proposition <a href="#prop:local-dimension" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:local-dimension">7</a> gives $$S_{i,0}=I_{v_i},\qquad S_i\subset D_{v_i},
 \qquad \dim I_{v_i}\geq s-1\geq1,$$ and $v_i$ has equality in <a href="#eq:local-abhyankar" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-abhyankar">[eq:local-abhyankar]</a>. We first show that $H$ is valuative. If, for example, $v_1\leq v_2$, then $$0\ne I_{v_1}\subset S_1\cap S_2=H,$$ so $H=I_{v_1}$. The other comparable case is symmetric. If the valuations are incomparable, Lemma <a href="#lem:local-approximation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:local-approximation">6</a> puts $H$ inside the inertia of their finest common coarsening. In either case every element of $H$ is valuative. Because it lies in each $S_i$, it then lies in each $S_{i,0}$, giving $$H=I_{v_1}\cap I_{v_2}.$$

Let $u=v_H$. By Theorem <a href="#thm:local-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:local-input">2</a>, it coarsens each $v_i$, and therefore $$H\subset I_u\subset I_{v_1}\cap I_{v_2}=H.$$ Equality in <a href="#eq:local-abhyankar" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-abhyankar">[eq:local-abhyankar]</a> passes to $u$. Its relative value group is free, so $\dim I_u=1$ implies $uF/u\kappa\cong\mathbb Z$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(Fu/\kappa u)=s-1$. If a coarsening $u'\leq u$ still has relative rank one, its one-dimensional inertia is contained in $I_u$, hence equals $H$. The defining coarseness of $v_H=u$ then gives $u\leq u'$, so $u=u'$. Thus $u$ is quasi-divisorial.

For uniqueness, if $v$ is any quasi-divisor with $I_v=H$, its associated coarsening $v_H$ has the same inertia. Equality in <a href="#eq:local-abhyankar" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-abhyankar">[eq:local-abhyankar]</a> passes to that coarsening, so it too has relative rank one and residue transcendence degree $s-1$. Minimality of $v$ forces $v=v_H$.

Finally, an element of $D_v$ is alternating with $h$ by Lemma <a href="#lem:residue-duality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:residue-duality">5</a>, since $h$ has zero residue image. Conversely, an element alternating with $h$ belongs to $D_{v_H}=D_v$ by Theorem <a href="#thm:local-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:local-input">2</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-local-flag-uniqueness"></a>
<div id="lem:local-flag-uniqueness" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9**. *If $v$ is a quasi-prime $r$-divisor, with $r\geq1$, then its value group has no nonzero $\ell$-divisible convex subgroup. Moreover $v=v_{I_v}$; in particular its inertia determines the valuation.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-local-flag-uniqueness"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-local-flag-uniqueness</summary>

*Proof.* First suppose $r=1$. An $\ell$-divisible convex subgroup $C$ of $vF$ maps to zero in $vF/v\kappa\cong\mathbb Z$, and hence lies in $v\kappa$. Coarsening by $C$ therefore leaves the relative value group unchanged. Lemma <a href="#lem:local-abhyankar" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:local-abhyankar">3</a> shows that the residue transcendence degree remains $s-1$, so minimality of $v$ forces $C=0$.

For a composite flag, the value group of its final quasi-prime step is a nonzero convex subgroup $B\subset vF$ with the property just proved.
